This rule opens the Anāgatakālikappaccayantanaya, the suffixes of the future, which Buddhappiya makes of the run §651–§655 of Kaccāyana's Uṇādikappa: [648 (§652): Kiriyāyaṃ ṇvutavo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r648), [649 (§654): Kammani ṇo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r649) and [650 (§655): Sese ssaṃ ntu mānānā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r650) follow it, while Kaccāyana's §653, which prescribes an inflection and not a suffix, he had already placed among the `kāraka` rules as [306 (§653): Bhāvavācimhi catutthī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r306). The section is introduced by the note that `kāle` governs, and closed by the `uṇādi` suffixes. `ṇī` is the suffix of habit of [590 (§532): Tassīlādīsu ṇītvāvī ca](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r590), here with its sense turned to the future.

Where the Kaccāyana vutti glosses each word with the full clause `āyatiṃ gamituṃ sīlaṃ yassa, so hotīti gāmī`, Buddhappiya explains the machinery: the `ṇ` of both suffixes is a marker for `vuddhi` (`ṇakārā vuddhatthā`, as [553 (§621): Kāritaṃ viya ṇānubandho](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r553) lays down) and is elided; the `gh` of `ghiṇ` is elided by [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517), so that the second suffix is in effect a short `i`, and he gives the inflected forms of both — `gāmī`, `gāmino` like `daṇḍī`, `gāmi`, `gāmī`, `gāmayo` like `aggi` — with the sentence `gāmaṃ gāmi`, "one who will go to the village", and `āgāmī kālo`, "the time to come". For `bhāji` he explains why the `j` is not turned into `g`: [571 (§618): Na kagattaṃ cajā ṇvumhi](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r571), split so that `ṇvumhi` is left off, forbids the replacement that [§640 (0): Sacajānaṃ kagā ṇānubandhe](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m640) — his own [554 (§623): Kagā cajānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r554) — would otherwise make before a `ṇ`-marked suffix. For `passāvi` he notes the `āva` of [§515 (541): Te āvāyā kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m515), "as in the causative", and for `paṭṭhāyi` the `āya` of [564 (§593): Ākārantānamāyo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r564).

There is no option marker; `bhavissati` is handed on as the governing word of the rules that follow.

:::

> Bhavissati kāle gammamāne gamā dīhi dhātūhi ṇī ghiṇa iccete paccayā honti. Ṇa kārā vuddhatthā. Āyati gamanaṃ sīlamassāti atthe ṇī, vuddhiṇa lopā. Gāmī, gāmino, āgāmī kālo. Ghiṇa paccaye – ‘‘kvaci dhātū’’tiādinā gha lopo, gāmaṃ gāmi, gāmī, gāmayo.

When future time is understood, after the roots `gamu` and the like the suffixes `ṇī` and `ghiṇ` are added. The `ṇ`'s are for the sake of `vuddhi`. In the sense "going in the future is his habit", `ṇī`; `vuddhi` and the elision of the `ṇ`: `gāmī`, "one who will go"; `gāmino`; `āgāmī kālo`, "the time to come". Before the suffix `ghiṇ`: the `gh` is elided by [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517) — `gāmaṃ gāmi`, "one who will go to the village"; `gāmī`, `gāmayo`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√gamu + ṇī) | gāmī | one who will go |
| 🚹①⨂(√gamu + ṇī) | gāmino | those who will go |
| 🚹①⨀(ā + √gamu + ṇī) | āgāmī kālo | the time to come |
| 🚹①⨀(√gamu + ghiṇ) | gāmaṃ gāmi | one who will go to the village |
| 🚹①⨂(√gamu + ghiṇ) | gāmī, gāmayo | those who will go |

|     | gamu + ṇī                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | g + (a→ā) + m + (~~ṇ~~ī)      | §621, §523 |
| →   | gāmī + (~~si~~)               | §220 |
| →   | **gāmī**                      |      |

|     | gamu + ghiṇ                   | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | g + (a→ā) + m + (~~gh~~i~~ṇ~~) | §517, §621, §523 |
| →   | gāmi + (~~si~~)               | §220 |
| →   | **gāmi**                      |      |

The `ṇ` works as it does in the causative, [§621 (553): Kāritaṃ viya ṇānubandho](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m621), and is dropped by [§523 (526): Kāritānaṃ ṇo lopaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m523). `gāmī` is inflected like `daṇḍī` (`gāmī`, `gāmino`), `gāmi` like `aggi` (`gāmi`; `gāmī`, `gāmayo`, by [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m88) and [§96 (148): Yosvakatarasso jho](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m96)).

:::

> Bhaja sevāyaṃ, āyati bhajituṃ sīlamassāti bhājī, bhāji, ‘‘na kagattaṃ cajā’’ti yogavibhāgena nisedhanato ‘‘sacajānaṃ kagā ṇānubandhe’’ti ga ttaṃ na bhavati.

`bhaja` in the sense of resorting to: "to resort in the future is his habit" — `bhājī`, `bhāji`; because of the prohibition [obtained] by splitting [§618 (571): Na ka gattaṃ ca jāṇvumhi](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m618) as "`na kagattaṃ cajā`", the `g` [for the `j`] by [§640 (0): Sacajānaṃ kagā ṇānubandhe](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m640) does not take place.

> Su gatimhi, kārite vuddhiāvā desā ca, āyati passavituṃ sīlamassāti passāvī, passāvi. Āyati paṭṭhānaṃ sīlamassāti paṭṭhāyī, paṭṭhāyi, ‘‘ākārantānamāyo’’ti āyā deso.

`su` in the sense of flowing: as in the causative, `vuddhi` and the replacement `āva` by [§515 (541): Te āvāyā kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m515): "to flow forth in the future is his habit" — `passāvī`, `passāvi`. "Setting out in the future is his habit" — `paṭṭhāyī`, `paṭṭhāyi`, [with] the replacement `āya` by [§593 (564): Ākārantānamāyo](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m593).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhaja + ṇī) | bhājī | one who will resort to |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhaja + ghiṇ) | bhāji | one who will resort to |
| 🚹①⨀(pa + √su + ṇī) | passāvī | one who will flow forth |
| 🚹①⨀(pa + √su + ghiṇ) | passāvi | one who will flow forth |
| 🚹①⨀(pa + √ṭhā + ṇī) | paṭṭhāyī | one who will set out |
| 🚹①⨀(pa + √ṭhā + ghiṇ) | paṭṭhāyi | one who will set out |

|     | bhaja + ṇī                    | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bh + (a→ā) + j + (~~a~~) + (~~ṇ~~ī) | §621, §83, §523 |
| →   | **bhājī**                     |      |

|     | pa + su + ghiṇ                | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pa + s + (u→o) + (~~gh~~i~~ṇ~~) | §621, §517, §523 |
| →   | pa + s + (o→āv) + i           | §515 |
| →   | pa + (s→ss) + āvi             | §28  |
| →   | **passāvi**                   |      |

|     | pa + ṭhā + ṇī                 | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pa + ṭh + (ā→āya) + (~~ṇ~~ī)  | §593, §523 |
| →   | pa + (ṭh→ṭṭh) + āyī           | §28  |
| →   | **paṭṭhāyī**                  |      |

In `bhājī` the `j` stays: the replacement `g` that a `ṇ`-marked suffix would bring ([§623 (554): Ka gā ca jānaṃ](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m623), [§640 (0): Sacajānaṃ kagā ṇānubandhe](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m640)) is blocked by Buddhappiya's reading of §618. The consonant after `pa` is doubled by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28).

:::

> ‘‘Bhavissatī’’ti adhikāro.

"`Bhavissati`" ("in the future") is the governing word [for the rules that follow].

---

<a name="r648"></a>

### 648 (§652): Kiriyāyaṃ ṇvutavo (`ṇvu`, `tu` when an action is the purpose, in the future sense) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§652 (648): Kriyāyaṃ ṇvu tavo" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m652" description="`ṇvu`, `tu` when an action is the purpose, in the future sense" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second rule of the future section, Kaccāyana's §652, with `bhavissati` carried from [647 (§651): Bhavissati gamādīhi ṇī ghiṇa](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r647). Buddhappiya prints the sutta as `Kiriyāyaṃ ṇvutavo`, with the vowel that Pāli inserts into `kriyā`, where the Kaccāyana heading has `Kriyāyaṃ`; the two are one word. The suffixes are the agent suffixes of [568 (§527): Sabbato ṇvu tvāvī vā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r568), and the agent noun so formed names its bearer by what he is about to do: `kārako vajati`, "the doer goes", means "he goes to do it".

His gloss turns the sutta's `kiriyāyaṃ` into `kiriyatthāyaṃ gammamānāyaṃ`, "when an action is understood as the purpose", and gives the derivations: for `ṇvu` the elision of the `ṇ`, `vuddhi` and the replacement `aka` of [570 (§622): Anakā yuṇvūnaṃ](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r570); for `tu` the `t` for the `r` of `kara` by [573 (§619): Karassa ca tattaṃ tusmiṃ](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r573), "the rest as `kattu`" — the agent noun inflected like `satthu` at [158 (§199): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r158). Where the Kaccāyana vutti explains the sense by a reported intention (`‘‘karissa’’nti kārako vajati`), Buddhappiya writes `karissaṃ vajatīti kārako vajati`, with the future participle `karissaṃ` that [650 (§655): Sese ssaṃ ntu mānānā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r650) will derive, and paraphrases the `tu` form by the infinitive, `kattā vajati` = `kattuṃ vajati`, "he goes in order to do" — the `tuṃ` of purpose he had derived at 637 by the `yogavibhāga` of 636. He adds `pācako`, `pacitā`, `bhuñjako`, `bhottā`.

There is no option marker.

:::

> Kiriyāyaṃ kiriyatthāyaṃ gammamānāyaṃ dhātūhi ṇvutu iccete paccayā honti bhavissati kāle. Ṇvu mhi – ṇa lopavuddhiakā desā, karissaṃ vajatīti kārako vajati.

When an action (`kiriyā`) is understood as the purpose, after roots the suffixes `ṇvu` and `tu` are added in the sense of future time. With `ṇvu`: the elision of the `ṇ`, `vuddhi` and the replacement `aka` by [§622 (570): Anakā yu ṇvūnaṃ](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m622) — "he goes [as one who] will do" (`karissaṃ vajati`): `kārako vajati`, "the doer goes".

> Tu mhi – ‘‘karassa ca tattaṃ tusmi’’nti ta kāro, sesaṃ kattusamaṃ, kattā vajati, kattuṃ vajatīti attho. Evaṃ pacissaṃ vajatīti pācako vajati, pacitā vajati. Bhuñjissaṃ vajatīti bhuñjako vajati, bhottā vajati iccādi.

With `tu`: `t` [for the `r` of `kara`] by [§619 (573): Karassa ca tattaṃ tusmiṃ](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m619); the rest is the same as `kattu`: `kattā vajati`, "the doer goes" — the meaning is `kattuṃ vajati`, "he goes in order to do". Likewise "he goes [as one who] will cook" (`pacissaṃ vajati`): `pācako vajati`, `pacitā vajati`, "the cook goes". "He goes [as one who] will eat" (`bhuñjissaṃ vajati`): `bhuñjako vajati`, `bhottā vajati`, "the eater goes"; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + ṇvu) | karissaṃ vajatīti kārako vajati | "he goes, about to do": the doer goes, i.e. he goes in order to do |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + tu) | kattā vajati | the doer goes (= `kattuṃ vajati`, he goes in order to do) |
| 🚹①⨀(√paca + ṇvu) | pacissaṃ vajatīti pācako vajati | "he goes, about to cook": the cook goes |
| 🚹①⨀(√paca + tu) | pacitā vajati | the cook goes |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + ṇvu) | bhuñjissaṃ vajatīti bhuñjako vajati | "he goes, about to eat": the eater goes |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + tu) | bhottā vajati | the eater goes |

|     | kara + ṇvu                    | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kara + (~~ṇ~~vu→aka)          | §523, §622 |
| →   | k + (a→ā) + r + (~~a~~) + aka | §621, §83 |
| →   | kāraka + (si→o)               | §104 |
| →   | **kārako**                    |      |

|     | kara + tu                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ka + (r→t) + tu               | §619 |
| →   | katt + (u→ā) + (~~si~~)       | §199 |
| →   | **kattā**                     |      |

|     | bhuja + tu                    | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bh + (u→o) + j + (~~a~~) + tu | §485, §83 |
| →   | bho + (~~j~~) + (t→tt) + u    | §578 |
| →   | bhott + (u→ā) + (~~si~~)      | §199 |
| →   | **bhottā**                    |      |

`pācako` has the `vuddhi` of the `ṇ` without a change of the `c`, by [§618 (571): Na ka gattaṃ ca jāṇvumhi](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m618); `pacitā` takes the augment `i` before `tu`; `bhuñjako` keeps the class sign of [§446 (509): Rudhādito niggahitapubbañca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m446), `bhottā` loses the `j` and doubles the `t` by [§578 (560): Bhujādīnamanto no dvi ca](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m578). The `tu` nouns take `ā` for their final before `si`, which is elided, by [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m199).

:::

---

<a name="r649"></a>

### 649 (§654): Kammani ṇo (`ṇa` with the object as first member, in the future sense) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§654 (649): Kammani ṇo" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m654" description="`ṇa` with the object as first member, in the future sense" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third future rule, Kaccāyana's §654 (his §653 having gone to the Kārakakaṇḍa as [306 (§653): Bhāvavācimhi catutthī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r306)); `bhavissati` continues from 647, and the closing `‘‘kammanī’’ti vattate` hands `kammani` on to [650 (§655): Sese ssaṃ ntu mānānā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r650). The suffix is the `ṇa` with which Buddhappiya opened the `kita` suffixes at [561 (§524): Dhātuyā kammādimhi ṇo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r561) and which [546 (§550): Ṇādayo tekālikā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r546) makes valid for all three times; this rule gives the compound it forms the sense of a purpose still to be carried out.

His gloss reads `kammani` as `kammasmiṃ upapade`, "when the object is the preceding word", names the operations — the elision of the `ṇ` and `vuddhi` — and glosses the roots the Kaccāyana vutti only uses: `lū` in the sense of cutting (`sālilāvo`, with the `āva` of [§515 (541): Te āvāyā kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m515)), `vapa` in the sense of sowing seed (`dhaññavāpo`); `bhogadāyo` and `sindhupāyo` take the `āya` of [564 (§593): Ākārantānamāyo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r564). Each compound is paired with the future sentence it stands for: `nagaraṃ karissatīti nagarakāro vajati`, "'he will build a city': the city-builder goes".

There is no option marker.

:::

> Kammasmiṃ upapade dhātūhi ṇa ppaccayo hoti bhavissati kāle ṇa lopavuddhī. Nagaraṃ karissatīti nagarakāro vajati. Lū chedane, sāliṃ lavissatīti sālilāvo vajati. Vapa bījasantāne, dhaññaṃ vapissatīti dhaññavāpo vajati. Bhogaṃ dadissatīti bhogadāyo vajati, sindhuṃ pivissatīti sindhupāyo vajati iccādi.

When the object (`kamma`) is the preceding word (`upapada`), after roots the suffix `ṇa` is added in the sense of future time; the elision of the `ṇ` and `vuddhi` [follow]. "He will build a city" (`nagaraṃ karissati`): `nagarakāro vajati`, "the city-builder goes". `lū` in the sense of cutting: "he will reap the rice" (`sāliṃ lavissati`): `sālilāvo vajati`, "the rice-reaper goes". `vapa` in the sense of sowing seed: "he will sow grain" (`dhaññaṃ vapissati`): `dhaññavāpo vajati`, "the grain-sower goes". "He will give wealth" (`bhogaṃ dadissati`): `bhogadāyo vajati`, "the wealth-giver goes"; "he will drink from the river" (`sindhuṃ pivissati`): `sindhupāyo vajati`, "the river-drinker goes"; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

In each pair the first sentence is the future verb with its object in the second form ②, the second the compound that replaces it, the subject of `vajati`, "he goes": he goes in order to build, reap, sow, give, drink.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(nagara + √kara + ṇa) | nagaraṃ karissatīti nagarakāro vajati | "he will build a city": the city-builder goes |
| 🚹①⨀(sāli + √lū + ṇa) | sāliṃ lavissatīti sālilāvo vajati | "he will reap the rice": the rice-reaper goes |
| 🚹①⨀(dhañña + √vapa + ṇa) | dhaññaṃ vapissatīti dhaññavāpo vajati | "he will sow grain": the grain-sower goes |
| 🚹①⨀(bhoga + √dā + ṇa) | bhogaṃ dadissatīti bhogadāyo vajati | "he will give wealth": the wealth-giver goes |
| 🚹①⨀(sindhu + √pā + ṇa) | sindhuṃ pivissatīti sindhupāyo vajati | "he will drink from the river": the river-drinker goes |

|     | nagara + kara + ṇa            | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | nagara + k + (a→ā) + r + (~~a~~) + (~~ṇ~~a) | §621, §83, §523 |
| →   | nagarakāra + (si→o)           | §104 |
| →   | **nagarakāro**                |      |

|     | sāli + lū + ṇa                | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sāli + l + (ū→o) + (~~ṇ~~a)   | §621, §523 |
| →   | sāli + l + (o→āv) + a         | §515 |
| →   | sālilāva + (si→o)             | §104 |
| →   | **sālilāvo**                  |      |

|     | bhoga + dā + ṇa               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bhoga + d + (ā→āya) + (~~ṇ~~a) | §593, §523 |
| →   | bhoga + dāy + (~~a~~) + a     | §83  |
| →   | bhogadāya + (si→o)            | §104 |
| →   | **bhogadāyo**                 |      |

The object's inflection is elided in the compound by [§317 (332): Tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ca](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m317), as for `kumbhakāro` at [563 (§624): Kattari kita](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r563); `dhaññavāpo` has the `vuddhi` of `vapa` alone.

:::

> ‘‘Kammanī’’ti vattate.

"`Kammani`" ("when the object is the preceding word") continues [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r650"></a>

### 650 (§655): Sese ssaṃ ntu mānānā (`ssaṃ`, `ntu`, `māna`, `āna` in the remaining [future] sense) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§655 (650): Sese ssaṃ ntumānānā" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m655" description="`ssaṃ`, `ntu`, `māna`, `āna` in the remaining [future] sense" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

This rule closes the Anāgatakālikappaccayantanaya, the four rules of the future that Buddhappiya sets after the present participles of [646 (§565): Vattamāne mānantā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r646) and before the `uṇādi` words: [647 (§651): Bhavissati gamādīhi ṇī ghiṇa](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r647) gives the agent by habit, [648 (§652): Kiriyāyaṃ ṇvutavo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r648) the agent who goes to do something, [649 (§654): Kammani ṇo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r649) the agent noun compounded with its object, and this rule the "remaining" future sense. He keeps Kaccāyana's order (§651, §652, §654, §655) and only lifts out §653, the rule on the fourth form with a word of action, which belongs to the Kārakakaṇḍa ([306 (§653): Bhāvavācimhi catutthī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r306)).

His gloss defines `sese` as `aparisamattatthe`, "in a sense not yet completed" — the action is under way or about to be done, not a habit and not a purpose — carries `kammani` down from 649 (`‘‘Kammanī’’ti vattate`) and `bhavissati` from 647, and adds that, being `kita` suffixes, the four denote the agent by [§624 (563): Kattari kita](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m624) ([563 (§624): Kattari kita](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r563)). Then come the derivations, which the Kaccāyana vutti does not give. `ssaṃ` takes the augment `i` of [§516 (466): Ikārāgamo asabbadhātukamhi](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m516), and the object does not compound with the participle as it did with `nagarakāro` at 649, `sāpekkhattā`, because the participle keeps its verbal dependence on it: `kammaṃ karissaṃ` stays two words. `ntu` takes the class-sign `o` of [§451 (520): Tanādito oyirā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m451) and the `a` for the final of `ntu` before `si` of [§94 (105): Simhi vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m94), and is inflected like `guṇavantu` ([98 (§124): Ā simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r98) onwards). A second analysis then reads `ssantu` as one suffix — the future sign and the participle together, as the `bhavissati` of the governing rule 647 itself suggests — and derives `karissaṃ` from it with `Simhi vā` and a `yogavibhāga` (splitting) of [§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m186): only its second half, `ntasaddo aṃ`, is applied, because `karissanta` is not one of the `gacchanta` words the whole rule names; the paradigm that follows is that of `gacchanta` at [108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r108). `māna` takes the `u` for the `o` and the `a` of `kara` by [§511 (521): Uttamokāro](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m511) and [§512 (522): Karassākāro ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m512). The general instruction that before `ntu` and `māna` each root takes its own class-sign (`sakasakavikaraṇappaccayo`) explains `karonto` with the `o` of the `tanu` class and `bhuñjanto` with the niggahīta of the `rudha` class ([§446 (509): Rudhādito niggahitapubbañca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m446)).

There is no option marker in the sutta and Buddhappiya reads none into it: the four suffixes are alternatives the sutta itself lists, each giving a good form, and within the `ntu` paradigm the doubled forms (`karissatā`, `karissantena`) are those the `vā` of the `ntu` rules allows.

:::

> Kammasmiṃ upapade sese aparisamattatthe dhātūhi ssaṃntu māna āna iccete paccayā honti bhavissati kāle gammamāne, te ca kitakattā kattari bhavanti. Kammaṃ karissatīti atthe ssaṃ paccayo, i kārāgamo, si lopo, kammaṃ karissaṃ vajati, sāpekkhattā na samāso. Ntu paccaye ‘‘tanādito oyirā’’ti o, ‘‘simhi vā’’ti ntva’ntassa a ttaṃ, kammaṃ karissatīti kammaṃ karonto vajati iccādi guṇavantu samaṃ.

When the object stands as the preceding word, in the remaining sense — that is, where the sense is not yet completed — the suffixes `ssaṃ`, `ntu`, `māna` and `āna` are added to roots when future time is understood; and being `kita` [suffixes] they denote the agent. In the sense "he will do the work", the suffix `ssaṃ` [is added], the augment `i`, elision of `si`: `kammaṃ karissaṃ vajati`, "he goes, about to do the work"; because [the participle] still depends on [its object], there is no compound. Before the suffix `ntu`, `o` by [§451 (520): Tanādito oyirā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m451), and by [§94 (105): Simhi vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m94) the final of `ntu` becomes `a`: "he will do the work" — `kammaṃ karonto vajati`, "he goes, doing the work", and so on, the same as `guṇavantu`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + ssaṃ) | kammaṃ karissaṃ vajati | he goes, about to do the work |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + ntu) | kammaṃ karonto vajati | he goes, doing the work |

|     | kara + ssaṃ                   | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kar + (~~a~~) + (i) + ssaṃ    | §83, §516 |
| →   | karissaṃ + (~~si~~)           | §220 |
| →   | **karissaṃ**                  |      |

|     | kara + ntu + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kar + (~~a~~) + (o) + ntu + si | §451, §83 |
| →   | karo + nt(u→a) + si           | §94  |
| →   | karonta + (si→o)              | §104 |
| →   | **karonto**                   |      |

The root's final `a` yields to the augment and to the class-sign by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m83); the `si` after `karissaṃ` is elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m220), and after `karonta` becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104).

:::

> Atha vā ‘‘bhavissati gamādīhi ṇī ghiṇa’’ti ettha ‘‘bhavissatī’’ti vacanato ‘‘ssantu’’iti ekova paccayo daṭṭhabbo, tato ‘‘simhi vā’’ti a ttaṃ, ‘‘ntasaddo a’’mitiyogavibhāgena a mādeso, silopo, karissaṃ karissanto, karissantā, karissantaṃ, karissante, karissatā karissantena, karissantehi, karissato karissantassa, karissataṃ karissantānaṃ, karissatā, karissantehi, karissato karissantassa, karissataṃ karissantānaṃ, karissati karissante, karissantesūtiādi guṇavantu sadisaṃ neyyaṃ.

Or else: since [the governing rule] [§651 (647): Bhavissati gamādīhi ṇī ghiṇa](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m651) says `bhavissati`, `ssantu` is to be regarded as one single suffix; then by `Simhi vā` [its final becomes] `a`, by splitting the rule [§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m186) — [taking only] "`ntasaddo aṃ`" — [the `nta`] is replaced by `aṃ`, and `si` is elided: `karissaṃ`. The rest is to be worked out like `guṇavantu`:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | karissaṃ, karissanto | karissantā |
| ② | karissantaṃ | karissante |
| ③ | karissatā, karissantena | karissantehi |
| ④ | karissato, karissantassa | karissataṃ, karissantānaṃ |
| ⑤ | karissatā | karissantehi |
| ⑥ | karissato, karissantassa | karissataṃ, karissantānaṃ |
| ⑦ | karissati, karissante | karissantesu |

:::tip[Example]

|     | kara + ssantu + si            | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kar + (~~a~~) + (i) + ssantu + si | §83, §516 |
| →   | karissa + nt(u→a) + si        | §94  |
| →   | karissa + (nta→aṃ) + si       | §186 |
| →   | kariss + (~~a~~) + aṃ + (~~si~~) | §83, §220 |
| →   | **karissaṃ**                  |      |

The forms with `ta` (`karissatā`, `karissato`, `karissataṃ`, `karissati`) are those of [§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m127) and [§128 (104): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m128), the forms with `nta` those of [§123 (103): Ntassa se vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m123), exactly as in `gacchatā`, `gacchantena` at [108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r108).

:::

> Māna mhi – o kārā kārānaṃ u ttaṃ, kammaṃ karissatīti kammaṃ kurumāno, kammaṃ karāno vajati. Evaṃ bhojanaṃ bhuñjissaṃ vajati, bhojanaṃ bhuñjanto, bhuñjamāno, bhuñjāno vajati.

Before `māna`, the `o` [of the class-sign] and the `a` [of `kara`] become `u`: "he will do the work" — `kammaṃ kurumāno`, `kammaṃ karāno vajati`, "he goes, doing the work". Likewise `bhojanaṃ bhuñjissaṃ vajati`, "he goes, about to eat the meal"; `bhojanaṃ bhuñjanto`, `bhuñjamāno`, `bhuñjāno vajati`, "he goes, eating the meal".

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + māna) | kammaṃ kurumāno vajati | he goes, doing the work |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + āna) | kammaṃ karāno vajati | he goes, doing the work |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + ssaṃ) | bhojanaṃ bhuñjissaṃ vajati | he goes, about to eat the meal |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + ntu) | bhojanaṃ bhuñjanto vajati | he goes, eating the meal |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + māna) | bhojanaṃ bhuñjamāno vajati | he goes, eating the meal |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + āna) | bhojanaṃ bhuñjāno vajati | he goes, eating the meal |

|     | kara + māna + si              | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kar + (~~a~~) + (o) + māna + si | §451, §83 |
| →   | k(a→u)r(o→u) + māna + si      | §512, §511 |
| →   | kurumāna + (si→o)             | §104 |
| →   | **kurumāno**                  |      |

`bhuñjanto` and `bhuñjamāno` show the niggahīta class-sign of the `rudha` class ([§446 (509): Rudhādito niggahitapubbañca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m446)) turned into `ñ` by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31); `karāno` and `bhuñjāno` are the `āna` forms, which the Tipiṭaka does not use.

> `tatra ṭhito tadadhimutto tabbahulavihārī aparihīno kālaṃ kurumāno` \
> [(15A3 3.2.4: Āneñjasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/15A3/3/3.2/3.2.4#916) \
🔼(tatra) 🚹①⨀(ṭhita) 🚹①⨀(tadadhimutta) 🚹①⨀(tabbahulavihārī) 🚹①⨀(aparihīna) 🚹②⨀(kāla) 🚹①⨀(√kara + māna) \
there | established | intent on it | dwelling much in it | without falling away | his time | making — that is, dying

> `na bhuñjamāno sabbaṃ hatthaṃ mukhe pakkhipissāmi` \
> [(2V 1.7.5.2: Dutiyaanāhaṭasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.7/1.7.5/1.7.5.2#1895) \
🔼(na) 🚹①⨀(√bhuja + māna) 🚹②⨀(sabba) 🚹②⨀(hattha) 🚹⑦⨀(mukha) ⏯👆⨀(pakkhipati) \
not | while eating | the whole | hand | into the mouth | I will put

:::

> Sabbattha kattari ntumāne su sakasakavikaraṇappaccayo kātabbo.

Everywhere, in the active, before `ntu` and `māna` each root's own class-sign (`vikaraṇa`) is to be applied.

> Khādanaṃ khādissatīti khādanaṃ khādissaṃ vajati, khādanaṃ khādanto, khādanaṃ khādamāno, khādanaṃ khādāno vajati. Maggaṃ carissatīti maggaṃ carissaṃ, maggaṃ caranto, maggaṃ caramāno, maggaṃ carāno vajati. Bhikkha āyācane, bhikkhaṃ bhikkhissatīti bhikkhaṃ bhikkhissaṃ carati, bhikkhaṃ bhikkhanto, bhikkhaṃ bhikkhamāno, bhikkhaṃ bhikkhāno carati iccādi.

"He will eat the food" — `khādanaṃ khādissaṃ vajati`, "he goes, about to eat the food"; `khādanaṃ khādanto`, `khādanaṃ khādamāno`, `khādanaṃ khādāno vajati`, "he goes, eating the food". "He will walk the road" — `maggaṃ carissaṃ`, `maggaṃ caranto`, `maggaṃ caramāno`, `maggaṃ carāno vajati`, "he goes, about to walk, walking the road". `bhikkha` in the sense of begging: "he will beg alms" — `bhikkhaṃ bhikkhissaṃ carati`, "he goes about, about to beg alms"; `bhikkhaṃ bhikkhanto`, `bhikkhaṃ bhikkhamāno`, `bhikkhaṃ bhikkhāno carati`, "he goes about begging alms"; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√khāda + ssaṃ) | khādanaṃ khādissaṃ vajati | he goes, about to eat the food |
| 🚹①⨀(√khāda + ntu) | khādanaṃ khādanto vajati | he goes, eating the food |
| 🚹①⨀(√khāda + māna) | khādanaṃ khādamāno vajati | he goes, eating the food |
| 🚹①⨀(√khāda + āna) | khādanaṃ khādāno vajati | he goes, eating the food |
| 🚹①⨀(√cara + ssaṃ) | maggaṃ carissaṃ vajati | he goes, about to walk the road |
| 🚹①⨀(√cara + ntu) | maggaṃ caranto vajati | he goes, walking the road |
| 🚹①⨀(√cara + māna) | maggaṃ caramāno vajati | he goes, walking the road |
| 🚹①⨀(√cara + āna) | maggaṃ carāno vajati | he goes, walking the road |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhikkha + ssaṃ) | bhikkhaṃ bhikkhissaṃ carati | he goes about, about to beg alms |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhikkha + ntu) | bhikkhaṃ bhikkhanto carati | he goes about, begging alms |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhikkha + māna) | bhikkhaṃ bhikkhamāno carati | he goes about, begging alms |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhikkha + āna) | bhikkhaṃ bhikkhāno carati | he goes about, begging alms |

These roots belong to the `bhū` class, whose class-sign `a` ([§445 (433): Bhūvādito a](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m445)) merges with the root's final: `khāda` + `a` + `ntu` is `khādanto`.

:::

---

> _Anāgatakālikappaccayantanayo._

Thus ends the treatment of [words] ending in the suffixes of future time.

---

## Uṇādippaccayantanaya (The `uṇādi` suffixes)

> Atha uṇā dayo vuccante.

Now `uṇa` and the rest — the `uṇādi` suffixes — are stated.

> ‘‘Dhātuyā’’ti adhikāro.

"`Dhātuyā`" (after a root) is the governing word, carried from [561 (§524): Dhātuyā kammādimhi ṇo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r561).

---

<a name="r651"></a>

### 651 (§650): Kāle vattamānātīte ṇvādayo (`ṇu` and the rest in present and past time) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§650 (651): Kāle vattamānātīte ṇvādayo" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m650" description="`ṇu` and the rest in present and past time" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

This rule opens the Uṇādippaccayantanaya, the sixth and last group of Buddhappiya's `kita` suffixes — his closing verse counts `tabba` and the rest, `ṇa` and the rest, the `niṭṭhā` participles, `tave` and `tuna` and the rest, `māna` and `anta` and the rest, and the `uṇādi`. The name is the Sanskrit grammarians': Pāṇini's `uṇādi` rules open with the suffix `uṇ`, and Buddhappiya's introductory sentence writes it `uṇa` (`Atha uṇādayo vuccante`), while Kaccāyana's first suffix is the same letters in the other order, `ṇu`. Kaccāyana's Uṇādikappa (§624–§673) began with `Kattari kita` and the `kicca` and `kta` rules, which Buddhappiya has already used at [563 (§624): Kattari kita](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r563), [605 (§625): Bhāvakammesu kiccaktakkhatthā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r605), [634 (§626): Kammani dutiyāyaṃ kto](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r634) and [635 (§643): Bhyādīhi matibudhipūjādīhi ca kto](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r635), and it placed the time rules §650–§655 in the middle; Buddhappiya opens with §650, the one `uṇādi` rule that assigns a time, because the future set §651–§655 has just been dealt with (647–650), and then works through §627–§649 and §656–§673 in Kaccāyana's order, closing with the `kāra` rule §604 from the Kibbidhānakappa. The governing word `dhātuyā`, "after a root", is restated from [561 (§524): Dhātuyā kammādimhi ṇo](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r561).

Where Kaccāyana's vutti gives three words, Buddhappiya gives some forty, each with its root and the root's meaning in the manner of the root lists (`Kara karaṇe`), a gloss-sentence and the operations that shape it, and he spells out that `ādi` covers `yu`, `kta` and `mi`. The `ṇ` of `ṇu` is a marker: it is elided and calls for `vuddhi` as a causative sign does ([§621 (553): Kāritaṃ viya ṇānubandho](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m621), [§523 (526): Kāritānaṃ ṇo lopaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m523), [§483 (527): Asaṃyogantassa vuddhi kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m483)), so that `kara` gives `kāru`; a root in `ā` takes `āya` before it by [§593 (564): Ākārantānamāyo](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m593) (`vāyu`); `tālu` has `l` for `r`. For `maru`, `taru` and the rest he withholds the `vuddhi` "by the word `vā` in [§484 (542): Ghaṭādīnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m484)": the `vā` that in Kaccāyana makes the `vuddhi` of `ghaṭa` and its group optional before the causative is extended to every `ṇ`-marked suffix, so that `vuddhi` becomes optional and usage settles where it applies — `kāru`, `dāru`, `sānu` with it, `maru`, `taru`, `tanu` without. He does not name the device, but it works as a `vavatthitavibhāsā` whose domains are the words themselves (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.2), and he appeals to it again as `vādhikārato` at [652 (§627): Khyādīhi mana ma ca to vā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r652) and [679 (§669): Munādīhi ci](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r679). `yu` becomes `ana` by [§641 (572): Nudādīhi yuṇvūnamanānanākānanakā sakāritehi ca](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m641) ([572 (§641): Nudādīhi yuṇvūnamanānanākānanakā sakāritehi ca](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r572)), the marker `i` of `cadi` brings a niggahīta, `bhū` becomes `bhuv` before the vowel by [§70 (30): Jhalānamiyuvā sare vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m70), and the `k` of `kta` is dropped, as at 634, under [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517).

The sutta has no option marker: the three suffixes are simply assigned to the two times. The only optionality in Buddhappiya's treatment is the borrowed `vā` that governs the `vuddhi`.

:::

> Atīte kāle, vattamāne ca gammamāne dhātūhi ṇuppaccayo hoti. Ādi saddena yu kta mi iccādayo ca honti.

When past time, and present [time], is understood, the suffix `ṇu` is added to roots. By the word `ādi` (and the rest), `yu`, `kta`, `mi` and the like [are added] as well.

> Kara karaṇe, akāsi, karotīti vā atthe ṇuppaccayo, ṇa lopo, vuddhi, kāru sippī, kārū kāravo. Vā gatigandhanesu, avāyi, vāyatīti vā vāyu, āyā deso. Sada assādane, assādīyatīti sādu. Rādha, sādha saṃsiddhimhi, sādhīyati anena hitanti sādhu. Bandha bandhane, attani paraṃ bandhatīti bandhu. Cakkha viyattiyaṃ vācāyaṃ, cakkhatīti cakkhu. I gatimhi, enti gacchanti pavattanti sattā etenāti āyu. Dara vidāraṇe, darīyatīti dāru kaṭṭhaṃ. Sanu dāne. Sanotīti sānu pabbatekadeso. Janīyatīti jānu jaṅghāsandhi. Carīyatīti cāru dassanīyo. Raha cāge, rahīyatīti rāhu asurindo. Tara taraṇe, tālu, lo ra ssa.

`kara` in the sense of doing: in the sense "he made" (`akāsi`) or "he makes" (`karoti`), the suffix `ṇu`; elision of the `ṇ`, `vuddhi`: `kāru`, a craftsman; `kārū`, `kāravo` [in the plural]. `vā` in the senses of going and of wafting: "it blew" (`avāyi`) or "it blows" (`vāyati`) — `vāyu`, wind; the replacement [of the final `ā`] by `āya`. `sada` in the sense of relishing: "it is relished" (`assādīyati`) — `sādu`, sweet. `rādha`, `sādha` in the sense of accomplishing: "welfare is accomplished (`sādhīyati`) by him" — `sādhu`, good. `bandha` in the sense of binding: "he binds another to himself" — `bandhu`, a kinsman. `cakkha` in the sense of distinct speech: "it declares (`cakkhati`)" — `cakkhu`, the eye. `i` in the sense of going: "beings go (`enti`), move, carry on by means of it" — `āyu`, life. `dara` in the sense of splitting: "it is split (`darīyati`)" — `dāru`, wood. `sanu` in the sense of giving: "it gives (`sanoti`)" — `sānu`, a part of a mountain, a ridge. "It is produced (`janīyati`)" — `jānu`, the knee, the joint of the leg. "It is resorted to (`carīyati`)" — `cāru`, lovely, fit to be looked at. `raha` in the sense of forsaking: "he is forsaken (`rahīyati`)" — `rāhu`, the lord of the asuras. `tara` in the sense of crossing: `tālu`, the palate, with `l` for the `r`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√kara + ṇu) | kāru | a craftsman, "he made, he makes" |
| 🚹①⨂(√kara + ṇu) | kārū, kāravo | craftsmen |
| 🚹①⨀(√vā + ṇu) | vāyu | wind, "it blew, it blows" |
| 🚹①⨀(√sada + ṇu) | sādu | sweet, "it is relished" |
| 🚹①⨀(√sādha + ṇu) | sādhu | good, "welfare is accomplished by him" |
| 🚹①⨀(√bandha + ṇu) | bandhu | a kinsman, "he binds another to himself" |
| 🚻①⨀(√cakkha + ṇu) | cakkhu | the eye, "it declares" |
| 🚻①⨀(√i + ṇu) | āyu | life, "beings carry on by it" |
| 🚻①⨀(√dara + ṇu) | dāru | wood, "it is split" |
| 🚹①⨀(√sanu + ṇu) | sānu | a mountain ridge, "it gives" |
| 🚻①⨀(√jana + ṇu) | jānu | the knee, "it is produced" |
| 🚹①⨀(√cara + ṇu) | cāru | lovely, "it is resorted to" |
| 🚹①⨀(√raha + ṇu) | rāhu | Rāhu, lord of the asuras, "he is forsaken" |
| 🚻①⨀(√tara + ṇu) | tālu | the palate |

|     | kara + ṇu                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | k(a→ā)ra + (~~ṇ~~)u           | §621, §483, §523 |
| →   | kār + (~~a~~) + u             | §83  |
| →   | kāru + (~~si~~)               | §220 |
| →   | **kāru**                      |      |

|     | vā + ṇu                       | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | v(ā→āya) + (~~ṇ~~)u           | §593, §523 |
| →   | vāy + (~~a~~) + u             | §83  |
| →   | **vāyu**                      |      |

`kārū` and `kāravo` are the first form plural, made as `bhikkhū` and `bhikkhavo` are by [§118 (146): Ghapato ca yonaṃ lopo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m118) with [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m88), and by [§119 (155): Lato vokāro ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m119). `bandhu` and `cakkhu` take no `vuddhi`, their roots ending in a conjunct ([§483 (527): Asaṃyogantassa vuddhi kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m483)); `āyu` has the root `i` strengthened to `e` and then `āy` before the vowel ([§515 (541): Te āvāyā kārite](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m515)).

> `cakkhu ceva rūpā ca, sotañca saddā ca` \
> [(11M 2.2: Chabbisodhanasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/11M/2/2.2#264) \
🚻①⨀(√cakkha + ṇu) 🔼(ca) 🔼(eva) 🚻①⨂(rūpa) 🔼(ca) 🚻①⨀(√su + ta) 🔼(ca) 🚹①⨂(sadda) 🔼(ca) \
the eye | and | indeed | forms | and | the ear | and | sounds | and

> `dāru dārutthikena pariyesitabbaṃ` \
> [(4V 12.1: Paṭhamabhāṇavāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/12/12.1#2237) \
🚻①⨀(√dara + ṇu) 🚹③⨀(dārutthika) 🚻①⨀(pariyesitabba) \
wood | by one who needs wood | is to be sought

:::

> Marā dīnaṃ panettha ṇu mhi ‘‘ghaṭādīnaṃ vā’’ti ettha vā saddena na vuddhi, maru, taru, tanu, dhanu, hanu, manu, asu, vasu, vaṭu, garu iccādi.

For `mara` and the rest, however, there is here no `vuddhi` before `ṇu`, by the word `vā` in [§484 (542): Ghaṭādīnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m484): `maru`, `taru`, `tanu`, `dhanu`, `hanu`, `manu`, `asu`, `vasu`, `vaṭu`, `garu`, and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√mara + ṇu) | maru | a god; a sandy waste |
| 🚹①⨀(√tara + ṇu) | taru | a tree |
| 🚹①⨀(√tanu + ṇu) | tanu | the body; slender |
| 🚻①⨀(√dhana + ṇu) | dhanu | a bow |
| 🚺①⨀(√hana + ṇu) | hanu | the jaw |
| 🚹①⨀(√mana + ṇu) | manu | a human being; Manu |
| 🚹①⨀(√asa + ṇu) | asu | breath, life |
| 🚻①⨀(√vasa + ṇu) | vasu | wealth |
| 🚹①⨀(√vaṭa + ṇu) | vaṭu | a lad |
| 🚹①⨀(√gara + ṇu) | garu | a teacher; heavy |

|     | tara + ṇu                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tara + (~~ṇ~~)u               | §523, §484 |
| →   | tar + (~~a~~) + u             | §83  |
| →   | **taru**                      |      |

The same root `tara` gave `tālu` above with `vuddhi` and `l` for `r`, and here `taru` without either: the two words show what the borrowed `vā` means — the `vuddhi` of the `ṇ`-marked suffix is settled word by word.

:::

> Cadi hilādane, yu ppaccayo, ‘‘nudādīhi yuṇvūnamanānanākānanakā sakāritehi cā’’ti anā deso, niggahītāgamo ca, candanaṃ. Bhavati etthāti bhuvanaṃ, ‘‘jhalānamiyuvāsare vā’’ti uvā deso. Kira vikkhepe, kiraṇo. Vicakkhaṇo, kampanaṃ karotīti karuṇā, a kārassu ttaṃ.

`cadi` in the sense of gladdening: the suffix `yu`, its replacement by `ana` by [§641 (572): Nudādīhi yuṇvūnamanānanākānanakā sakāritehi ca](/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m641), and the augment niggahīta: `candanaṃ`, sandalwood. "It comes to be (`bhavati`) here" — `bhuvanaṃ`, the world; the replacement by `uva` by [§70 (30): Jhalānamiyuvā sare vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m70). `kira` in the sense of scattering: `kiraṇo`, a ray. `vicakkhaṇo`, discerning. "It causes trembling (`kampanaṃ karoti`)" — `karuṇā`, compassion; the `a` becomes `u`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(√cadi + yu) | candanaṃ | sandalwood, "it gladdens" |
| 🚻①⨀(√bhū + yu) | bhuvanaṃ | the world, "it comes to be here" |
| 🚹①⨀(√kira + yu) | kiraṇo | a ray, "it scatters" |
| 🚹①⨀(vi + √cakkha + yu) | vicakkhaṇo | discerning, "he sees distinctly" |
| 🚺①⨀(√kara + yu) | karuṇā | compassion, "it causes trembling" |

|     | cadi + yu                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ca(ṃ)d + (~~i~~) + (yu→ana)   | §517, §641 |
| →   | ca(ṃ→n)dana + (si→aṃ)         | §31, §219 |
| →   | **candanaṃ**                  |      |

|     | bhū + yu                      | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bh(ū→uv) + (yu→ana)           | §70, §641 |
| →   | bhuvana + (si→aṃ)             | §219 |
| →   | **bhuvanaṃ**                  |      |

The marker `i` with which `cadi` is cited stands for a niggahīta before the root's final consonant, which then becomes the nasal of its group by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31); the neuter `si` becomes `aṃ` by [§219 (195): Siṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m219). `kiraṇo` and `karuṇā` have `ṇ` for the `n` of `ana` after the `r` of the root by [§549 (550): Rahādito ṇa](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m549), and `karuṇā` the feminine `ā` of [§237 (176): Itthīyamato āpaccayo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m237).

> `anujānāmi, bhikkhave, candanaṃ, tagaraṃ, kāḷānusāriyaṃ` \
> [(3V 6.2: Mūlādibhesajjakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/6/6.2#1213) \
▶️👆⨀(anujānāti) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚻②⨀(√cadi + yu) 🚻②⨀(tagara) 🚻②⨀(kāḷānusāriya) \
I allow | bhikkhus | sandalwood | tagara | black gum

:::

> Kta ppaccaye – ka lopo, abhavi, bhavatīti vā bhūtaṃ yakkhādi, bhūtāni. Vāyatīti vāto, tāyatīti tāto. Mi mhi – bhavanti etthāti bhūmi, netīti nemi iccādi.

Before the suffix `kta`: elision of the `k`; "it was" (`abhavi`) or "it is" (`bhavati`) — `bhūtaṃ`, a being, such as a yakkha; `bhūtāni`. "It blows (`vāyati`)" — `vāto`, wind; "he protects (`tāyati`)" — `tāto`, father. Before `mi`: "they come to be (`bhavanti`) on it" — `bhūmi`, the earth; "it leads (`neti`)" — `nemi`, the rim of a wheel; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(√bhū + kta) | bhūtaṃ | a being (a yakkha and the like), "it was, it is" |
| 🚻①⨂(√bhū + kta) | bhūtāni | beings |
| 🚹①⨀(√vā + kta) | vāto | wind, "it blows" |
| 🚹①⨀(√tā + kta) | tāto | father, "he protects" |
| 🚺①⨀(√bhū + mi) | bhūmi | the earth, "they come to be on it" |
| 🚺①⨀(√nī + mi) | nemi | the rim of a wheel, "it leads" |

|     | bhū + kta                     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | bhū + (~~k~~)ta               | §517 |
| →   | bhūta + (si→aṃ)               | §219 |
| →   | **bhūtaṃ**                    |      |

|     | nī + mi                       | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | n(ī→e) + mi                   | §485 |
| →   | nemi + (~~si~~)               | §220 |
| →   | **nemi**                      |      |

`vāto` and `tāto` keep the long vowel of their roots and take `o` for `si` by [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104); `nemi` takes the `vuddhi` `e` by [§485 (434): Aññesu ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m485).

:::

---

<a name="r652"></a>

### 652 (§627): Khyādīhi mana ma ca to vā (`mana` after `khi` and the like; and optionally `t` for its `m`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§627 (652): Khyādīhi mana ma ca to vā" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m627" description="`mana` after `khi` and the like; and optionally `t` for its `m`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Having placed the time rule first, Buddhappiya returns to the head of Kaccāyana's Uṇādikappa and now follows its order: §627–§628 (652–653), §630–§634 (654–658), §636 (659), §638 (660), §644–§645 (661–662), §647–§649 (663–665) and §656–§673 (666–683), leaving out the rules he has used elsewhere — §629 at [569 (§629): Gahassupadhasse vā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r569), §635 at [559 (§635): Pesātisaggapattakālesu kiccā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r559), §639, §641–§643 among the `kita` and past-participle rules, and §646 (`Ekādito sakissa kkhattuṃ`) in the Taddhitakaṇḍa (419) — and the two rules, §637 and §640, to which the Rūpasiddhi gives no sutta of their own. This is the first of the `uṇādi` rules proper, one suffix for a list of roots.

His gloss gives the list with `khī` (Kaccāyana's `khi`), and then a summary verse of the kind described in *Boundaries and Domains* §3.3, before the derivations. Each word has its root and root meaning, its gloss-sentence and its operations: the `na` of `mana` is dropped as a marker under [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517), the root vowel takes `vuddhi` by [§485 (434): Aññesu ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m485) (`khemo`, `somo`, `homo`, `hemo`, `lomo`, `pemo`), and in `bhīmo` the `vuddhi` is withheld `vādhikārato`, "by the governance of `vā`" — the `vā` of [§484 (542): Ghaṭādīnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m484) that he invoked at 651 — where Kaccāyana's vutti has `bhemo`. For `attā` he adds a further operation that Kaccāyana leaves unsaid, the `t` for the `d` of `ada` before the new `t`, quoting it in the form of a statement, `‘‘to dassā’’ti` (it is not a Kaccāyana sutta, but a `nipātana` of the kind he uses again at 666, `to dassa, pavittaṃ`), and for `ātumā` the lengthening and the augment `u` under §517; he adds `yāmo` to the list. The final `‘‘Vā’’ti vattamāne` carries the `vā` into the next rule, where Kaccāyana has none.

The option marker is the `vā` of `ma ca to vā`, and this is the sutta at which Buddhappiya states most plainly what a `vavatthitavibhāsā` is (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3, §3.2): the change of `m` to `t` is `nicca` for the root `ada` — only `attā` and `ātumā` exist, never a form with `m` — and `asanta` for every other root of the list, which keep `ma` (`khemo`, never `kheto`). There is no `anicca` domain here; the verse draws the boundary. The `ca` joins the second operation to the first.

:::

> Khī bhī su ru hu vā dhū hi lūpī ada iccevamādīhi dhātūhi mana paccayo hoti, ma ssa ca to hoti vā.

After the roots `khī`, `bhī`, `su`, `ru`, `hu`, `vā`, `dhū`, `hi`, `lū`, `pī`, `ada` and the like, the suffix `mana` is added; and its `m` optionally becomes `t`.

> Adadhātuparasseva, makārassa takāratā; \
> Tadaññato na hotāyaṃ, vavatthitavibhāsato.

Only after the root `ada` does the letter `m` become `t`; \
after any other [root] this does not take place — by the restricted option (`vavatthitavibhāsā`).

> Khī khaye, khīyanti e ttha upaddavupasaggādayoti atthe mana paccayo, ‘‘kvaci dhātū’’tiādinā na lopo, ‘‘aññesu cā’’ti vuddhi, khemo. Tathā bhī bhaye, bhāyanti etasmāti bhīmo, vā dhikārato na vuddhi. Su abhisave, savatīti somo. Ru gatimhi, romo. Hu dānādanahabyappadānesu, hūyatīti homo. Vā gatigandhanesu, vāmo. Dhū kampane, dhunātīti dhūmo. Hi gatimhi, hinotīti hemo. Lū chedane, lūyatīti lomo. Pī tappane, pīṇanaṃ pemo. Ada bhakkhaṇe, adatīti atthe mana, ma ssa ca vā ta kāro, ‘‘to dassā’’ti ta kāro, attā, ātumā, ‘‘kvaci dhātū’’tiādinā ada ssa dīgho, u kārāgamo ca. Yā pāpaṇe, yāmo.

`khī` in the sense of waning: in the sense "misfortunes, troubles and the like wane (`khīyanti`) here", the suffix `mana`; elision of the `na` by [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517), `vuddhi` by [§485 (434): Aññesu ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m485): `khemo`, safety. Likewise `bhī` in the sense of fearing: "they fear (`bhāyanti`) because of it" — `bhīmo`, terrible; no `vuddhi`, by the governance of `vā`. `su` in the sense of flowing: "it flows (`savati`)" — `somo`, the moon. `ru` in the sense of going: `romo`, body-hair. `hu` in the senses of giving, eating and offering an oblation: "it is offered (`hūyati`)" — `homo`, an oblation. `vā` in the senses of going and wafting: `vāmo`, left, contrary. `dhū` in the sense of shaking: "it shakes (`dhunāti`)" — `dhūmo`, smoke. `hi` in the sense of going: "it moves (`hinoti`)" — `hemo`, gold. `lū` in the sense of cutting: "it is cut (`lūyati`)" — `lomo`, hair. `pī` in the sense of pleasing: "pleasing (`pīṇanaṃ`)" — `pemo`, love. `ada` in the sense of eating: in the sense "it eats (`adati`)", `mana`, and optionally `t` for its `m`; by [the statement] "`t` for the `d`", `t`: `attā`, the self; `ātumā` — the lengthening of [the `a` of] `ada` and the augment `u` by §517. `yā` in the sense of reaching: `yāmo`, a watch [of the night].

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√khī + mana) | khemo | safety, "troubles wane here" |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhī + mana) | bhīmo | terrible, "they fear because of it" |
| 🚹①⨀(√su + mana) | somo | the moon, "it flows" |
| 🚹①⨀(√ru + mana) | romo | body-hair, "it goes" |
| 🚹①⨀(√hu + mana) | homo | an oblation, "it is offered" |
| 🚹①⨀(√vā + mana) | vāmo | left; contrary, "it goes the other way" |
| 🚹①⨀(√dhū + mana) | dhūmo | smoke, "it shakes" |
| 🚹①⨀(√hi + mana) | hemo | gold, "it moves" |
| 🚹①⨀(√lū + mana) | lomo | hair, "it is cut" |
| 🚹①⨀(√pī + mana) | pemo | love, "pleasing" |
| 🚹①⨀(√ada + mana) | attā | the self, "it eats" |
| 🚹①⨀(√ada + mana) | ātumā | the self |
| 🚹①⨀(√yā + mana) | yāmo | a watch of the night, "it reaches" |

|     | khī + mana + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | khī + ma(~~na~~) + si         | §517 |
| →   | kh(ī→e) + ma + si             | §485 |
| →   | khema + (si→o)                | §104 |
| →   | **khemo**                     |      |

|     | ada + mana + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ad + (~~a~~) + ma(~~na~~) + si | §83, §517 |
| →   | ad + (m→t)a + si              | §627 |
| →   | a(d→t)ta + si                 | `to dassa` |
| →   | atta + (si→ā)                 | §189 |
| →   | **attā**                      |      |

|     | ada + mana + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (a→ā)d + (~~a~~) + ma(~~na~~) + si | §517, §83 |
| →   | ā(d→t) + (u) + ma + si        | `to dassa`, §517 |
| →   | ātuma + (si→ā)                | §189 |
| →   | **ātumā**                     |      |

`attā` and `ātumā` take `ā` for `si` as members of the `atta` group of [§189 (113): Syā ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189); the rest take `o` by [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104). In `bhīmo`, `dhūmo`, `vāmo`, `yāmo` and `romo` there is no `vuddhi`.

> `dhajo rathassa paññāṇaṃ, dhūmo paññāṇamaggino` \
> [(12S1 1.8.2: Rathasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/1/1.8/1.8.2#338) \
🚹①⨀(dhaja) 🚹⑥⨀(√raha + tha) 🚻①⨀(paññāṇa) 🚹①⨀(√dhū + mana) 🚻①⨀(paññāṇa) 🚹⑥⨀(aggi) \
a banner | of a chariot | the mark | smoke | the mark | of a fire

:::

> ‘‘Vā’’ti vattamāne –

While "`vā`" is [still] in force —

<a name="r653"></a>

### 653 (§628): Samādihi thamā (`tha` and `ma` after `samu` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§628 (653): Samādīhi tha mā" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m628" description="`tha` and `ma` after `samu` and the like" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §628 in its own place, straight after §627. Buddhappiya's root list differs from the vutti's `samu damu dara raha du hi si bhī dā yā sā ṭhā bhasa`: he cites the roots without the marker `u` (`sama`, `dama`), drops `bhī` and `yā`, whose `bhīmo` and `yāmo` he has just derived at [652 (§627): Khyādīhi mana ma ca to vā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r652), and adds `sapa` (`sapatho`), `vasa` (`āvasatho`), `yu` (`yūtho`), `baha` (`brahmā`) and `usu` (`usmā`). Each word again has its root meaning and gloss; the operations he names are all licences of [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517) — and he draws attention to the word `kvaci` in that rule: because its scope is "in some places", the root's final is *not* elided in `samatho`, `damatho`, but is in `ratho` (`kvaciggahaṇādhikārā na dhātvantalopo`); `yūtho` and `sīmā` lengthen, `thāmo` turns `ṭh` into `th`, `bhasmā` and `brahmā` take `ā` for `si` as members of the `brahmā` group by [§189 (113): Syā ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189), and `brahmā` gets its `bra` for `ba` by direct statement (`nipātana`).

Kaccāyana's sutta has no option marker; Buddhappiya reads it with the `vā` of 652, which the closing words of that rule (`‘‘Vā’’ti vattamāne`) carry down, and his gloss ends `honti vā`, "are optionally added". He does not gloss the `vā` further, so it is an open option (`vikappa`, *Boundaries and Domains* §3.1): `tha` and `ma` are available to these roots beside the words they form with other suffixes — `samo`, `damo`, `daro` with the `a` of [599 (§553): Itthiyamatiyavo vā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r599), `davo` beside `dumo` — and which of the two suffixes a root takes is a matter of the attested word, not of the `vā`.

:::

> Samadamadara raha sapa[^80] vasa yu du hi si dā sā ṭhā bhasa bahausu iccevamādīhi dhātūhi tha ma iccete paccayā honti vā.

After the roots `sama`, `dama`, `dara`, `raha`, `sapa`, `vasa`, `yu`, `du`, `hi`, `si`, `dā`, `sā`, `ṭhā`, `bhasa`, `baha`, `usu` and the like, the suffixes `tha` and `ma` are optionally added.

> Sama upasame, kvaci ggahaṇādhikārā na dhātvantalopo, kilese sametīti samatho samādhi. Evaṃ damanaṃ damatho. Dara dāhe, daraṇaṃ daratho paridāho. Raha upādāne, rahīyatīti ratho, ‘‘kvaci dhātū’’tiādinā ha lopo. Sapa akkose, sapanaṃ sapatho. Vasa nivāse, āvasanti etasminti āvasatho. Yu missane, yūtho, dīgho. Du gativuddhimhi, davati vaḍḍhatīti dumo. Hinotīti himo ussāvo. Si bandhane, sīyatīti sīmā, dīgho. Dā avakhaṇḍane, dāmo. Sā sāmatthe, sāmo. Ṭhā gatinivattimhi, thāmo, ṭha ssa tha ttaṃ. Bhasa bhasmīkaraṇe, bhasmā, brahmā dittā ‘‘syā cā’’ti ā ttaṃ. Baha vuddhimhi, brahmā, nipātanato bro ba ssa. Usu dāhe, usmā iccādi.

`sama` in the sense of calming: by the governance of the word `kvaci` [in §517] the root's final is not elided; "it calms (`sameti`) the defilements" — `samatho`, calm, that is, concentration. Likewise "taming (`damanaṃ`)" — `damatho`, taming. `dara` in the sense of burning: "burning (`daraṇaṃ`)" — `daratho`, distress, a burning. `raha` in the sense of taking up: "it is taken up (`rahīyati`)" — `ratho`, a chariot; elision of the `h` by [§517 (488): Kvaci dhātuvibhattippaccayānaṃ dīghaviparītādesalopāgamā ca](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m517). `sapa` in the sense of cursing: "cursing (`sapanaṃ`)" — `sapatho`, an oath. `vasa` in the sense of dwelling: "they dwell (`āvasanti`) in it" — `āvasatho`, a dwelling. `yu` in the sense of mixing: `yūtho`, a herd; lengthening. `du` in the senses of going and growing: "it moves (`davati`), it grows" — `dumo`, a tree. "It moves (`hinoti`)" — `himo`, frost. `si` in the sense of binding: "it is bound (`sīyati`)" — `sīmā`, a boundary; lengthening. `dā` in the sense of cutting off: `dāmo`, a cord. `sā` in the sense of being able: `sāmo`, dark-hued. `ṭhā` in the sense of ceasing to move: `thāmo`, strength; `th` for the `ṭh`. `bhasa` in the sense of reducing to ashes: `bhasmā`, ashes; as a member of the `brahmā` group, `ā` [for `si`] by [§189 (113): Syā ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189). `baha` in the sense of growing: `brahmā`, Brahmā; `bra` for the `ba` by direct statement. `usu` in the sense of burning: `usmā`, heat; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(√sama + tha) | samatho | calm, concentration, "it calms the defilements" |
| 🚹①⨀(√dama + tha) | damatho | taming |
| 🚹①⨀(√dara + tha) | daratho | distress, "a burning" |
| 🚹①⨀(√raha + tha) | ratho | a chariot, "it is taken up" |
| 🚹①⨀(√sapa + tha) | sapatho | an oath, "cursing" |
| 🚹①⨀(ā + √vasa + tha) | āvasatho | a dwelling, "they dwell in it" |
| 🚹①⨀(√yu + tha) | yūtho | a herd, "mixing" |
| 🚹①⨀(√du + ma) | dumo | a tree, "it grows" |
| 🚹①⨀(√hi + ma) | himo | frost, "it moves" |
| 🚺①⨀(√si + ma) | sīmā | a boundary, "it is bound" |
| 🚹①⨀(√dā + ma) | dāmo | a cord, "it cuts off" |
| 🚹①⨀(√sā + ma) | sāmo | dark-hued, "he is able" |
| 🚹①⨀(√ṭhā + ma) | thāmo | strength, "it holds still" |
| 🚹①⨀(√bhasa + ma) | bhasmā | ashes, "it reduces to ashes" |
| 🚹①⨀(√baha + ma) | brahmā | Brahmā, "he grows" |
| 🚹①⨀(√usu + ma) | usmā | heat, "it burns" |

|     | sama + tha + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sama + tha + (si→o)           | §104 |
| →   | **samatho**                   |      |

|     | raha + tha + si               | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ra + (~~ha~~) + tha + si      | §517 |
| →   | ratha + (si→o)                | §104 |
| →   | **ratho**                     |      |

|     | baha + ma + si                | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ba→bra)h + (~~a~~) + ma + si | §571, §83 |
| →   | brahma + (si→ā)               | §189 |
| →   | **brahmā**                    |      |

The root-final `a` of `sama` stays before `tha` — this is the point of Buddhappiya's remark on `kvaci` — while `raha` loses its whole final syllable; `sīmā` takes the feminine `ā` of [§237 (176): Itthīyamato āpaccayo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m237), and `brahmā`, `bhasmā`, `usmā` the `ā` for `si` of [§189 (113): Syā ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189); the `bra` of `brahmā` is laid down by [§571 (624): Paccayādaniṭṭhā nipātanā sijjhanti](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m571).

> `yānaṃ nāma vayhaṃ ratho sakaṭaṃ sandamānikā` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#237) \
🚻①⨀(yāna) 🔼(nāma) 🚻①⨀(vayha) 🚹①⨀(√raha + tha) 🚻①⨀(sakaṭa) 🚺①⨀(sandamānikā) \
a vehicle | namely | a litter | a chariot | a cart | a carriage

> `atha kho brahmā sahampati abhikkantāya rattiyā` \
> [(3V 1.12: Uruvelapāṭihāriyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.12#177) \
🔼(atha) 🔼(kho) 🚹①⨀(√baha + ma) 🚹①⨀(sahampati) 🚺⑦⨀(abhikkanta) 🚺⑦⨀(√ranja + ti) \
then | indeed | Brahmā | Sahampati | when far advanced | the night

:::

[^80]: CST4 prints `lapa`; the example `Sapa akkose, sapanaṃ sapatho` shows that the root is `sapa`.
